# Proposed OEIS comments: A236052 to A238306

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A236052

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236052>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [6140](../papers/06001-06500/06140-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 7*a(n-2) +10*a(n-4) +15*a(n-6) -314*a(n-8) -1389*a(n-10) +1365*a(n-12) +3415*a(n-14) +2472*a(n-16) +2424*a(n-18) +3384*a(n-20) -6192*a(n-22) +1152*a(n-24) -2304*a(n-26) for n>29

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 13 716 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 29. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A236055

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236055>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2510](../papers/02501-03000/02510-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A236078

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236078>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4266](../papers/04001-04500/04266-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 82 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 140 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 82, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A236271

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236271>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [10062](../papers/10001-10500/10062-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) - 16*a(n-3) - 8*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(21 - x - 80*x^2 - 36*x^3) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x - 10*x^2 - 4*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 4 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A236407

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236407>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11184](../papers/11001-11500/11184-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n*(n-3)*a(n) -4*(2*n-1)*(n-3)*a(n-1) +2*(7*n^2-28*n+20)*a(n-2) -4*(n-1)*(2*n-7)*a(n-3) +(n-1)*(n-4)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Feb 01 2014

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [5904](../papers/05501-06000/05904-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 15*a(n-1) +15*a(n-2) -1145*a(n-3) +2387*a(n-4) +37971*a(n-5) -125157*a(n-6) -717925*a(n-7) +2961789*a(n-8) +8497725*a(n-9) -42143099*a(n-10) -64302155*a(n-11) +395479985*a(n-12) +292816593*a(n-13) -2542859159*a(n-14) -536734167*a(n-15) +11318363232*a(n-16) -2143673968*a(n-17) -34416928152*a(n-18) +18241618944*a(n-19) +68291364640*a(n-20) -59473522432*a(n-21) -78323431552*a(n-22) +105248727808*a(n-23) +31220975104*a(n-24) -96325931008*a(n-25) +27167551488*a(n-26) +30305468416*a(n-27) -24934612992*a(n-28) +7018905600*a(n-29) -707788800*a(n-30)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 29. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [5460](../papers/05001-05500/05460-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 16*a(n-1) -1222*a(n-3) +4306*a(n-4) +37240*a(n-5) -215317*a(n-6) -540094*a(n-7) +5203112*a(n-8) +2292844*a(n-9) -76124580*a(n-10) +48925344*a(n-11) +731455664*a(n-12) -1009008048*a(n-13) -4767540048*a(n-14) +9634148480*a(n-15) +21068679040*a(n-16) -58279824064*a(n-17) -60171290304*a(n-18) +241589450496*a(n-19) +89789142784*a(n-20) -704484707584*a(n-21) +42074520064*a(n-22) +1448865873408*a(n-23) -538005643264*a(n-24) -2069440729088*a(n-25) +1288382918656*a(n-26) +1977460977664*a(n-27) -1703477510144*a(n-28) -1169175658496*a(n-29) +1362600230912*a(n-30) +349325819904*a(n-31) -643081306112*a(n-32) -6231949312*a(n-33) +161110032384*a(n-34) -21835546624*a(n-35) -16116613120*a(n-36) +3464495104*a(n-37)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 58 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 36. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A237310

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A237310>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4154](../papers/04001-04500/04154-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 99 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 192 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 99, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A237315

Paper: [213](../papers/00001-00500/00213-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A238180>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [453](../papers/00001-00500/00453-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrences in the linked file are correct. Column k counts arrays of a fixed width, so it is a walk count in a finite graph and satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence; Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one, its order is the order stated for that column, and so is the order of every tail, so any recurrence of that order is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A238249

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A238249>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [1552](../papers/01501-02000/01552-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) +16*a(n-2) -82*a(n-3) -99*a(n-4) +774*a(n-5) +196*a(n-6) -4456*a(n-7) +1106*a(n-8) +17464*a(n-9) -10080*a(n-10) -49212*a(n-11) +40922*a(n-12) +102532*a(n-13) -108728*a(n-14) -159592*a(n-15) +207747*a(n-16) +184428*a(n-17) -296048*a(n-18) -153442*a(n-19) +318857*a(n-20) +83878*a(n-21) -259308*a(n-22) -19872*a(n-23) +157052*a(n-24) -10232*a(n-25) -68768*a(n-26) +12128*a(n-27) +20592*a(n-28) -5472*a(n-29) -3776*a(n-30) +1280*a(n-31) +320*a(n-32) -128*a(n-33)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 84 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 32. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A238250

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A238250>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [10727](../papers/10501-11000/10727-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 81 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 400 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 81, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A238287

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A238287>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2754](../papers/02501-03000/02754-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence for column k = 1 is true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
